# Config ledger: one verdict per config key

[`CONFIG_promotion_ledger.json`](CONFIG_promotion_ledger.json) is the ledger and the authority:
one row per config key the network loader reads (plus the few other-section keys a network
posture depends on) with its verdict, default, golden value, one-sentence reason and evidence
card. The text below the GENERATED marker is rendered from it by `scripts/ledger_render.py`. The
posture is the two goldens in `config/golden/`, generated by `scripts/make_golden_config.py` from
`golden_manifest.json` and this JSON, never hand-edited; a knob is armed only at the author's
decision through a ledger row and the generator. Topic page: [config-ledger](topics/config-ledger.md);
the user-facing knob reference is [CONFIG.md](CONFIG.md).

## How a ledger change is made

1. Edit the key's row in `docs/CONFIG_promotion_ledger.json` (a new key gets its row in the
   commit that adds it). To arm a knob, also pin it in `config/golden/golden_manifest.json` and
   run `python scripts/make_golden_config.py`.
2. `python scripts/ledger_render.py`.
3. `python scripts/make_golden_config.py --verify`: fails on an in-regime RECOMMEND_* row a golden
   leaves unarmed outside the manifest's `exceptions`, on a stale exception, on a pin that does
   not read back.
4. `python scripts/config_posture_check.py --verify-loader`: 0 gaps (RECOMMEND_* rows against the
   loader's posture table in both directions, predicate syntax, the `on()` trap).
5. A RECOMMEND_ON / RECOMMEND_SCOPED row needs `regime`, `posture_why`, `armed_when` for a value
   knob, `fingerprinted` for a fingerprint member, and a row testing the same predicate in
   `src/core/config/GnssConfigNetworkPosture.cpp` (`appendNetworkPostureItemsOn_` /
   `..Scoped_`); a demotion removes that row.
6. `python scripts/docs_index_check.py` before the commit.

## Machine fields

The loader's run-start POSTURE block, `config_posture_check.py <config> --tier all` (offline)
and `make_golden_config.py --verify` evaluate the same predicates.

**`regime`**: a row is reported only where it is true, because a knob validated at one scale can
harm at another (the retired `mw_wl.bridge_sample_debias`: good at 50 stations, wrong-integer
rate 0.0 % to 12.5 % at 305). `"True"` is unconditional; `"False"` marks a recommended key that is deliberately
not a posture row (`posture_why` says why); a RECOMMEND_* row without one is a gap.

| token | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `n_sta`, `n_sys` | station count; length of `processing.active_systems` |
| `tri` | `frequency_mode` triple or all |
| `warm` | resumed leg: the loader tests that `state_snapshot_path` is set, the checker that the snapshot exists (undecidable reads warm) |
| `rt` | real-time `app_mode` or `network.service_mode` |
| `pstations`, `par` | `parallel_stations` (default true); and `n_sta` > `parallel_min_stations` (default 10) |
| `gps_only`, `gps_gal_only` | constellation set by name (a count admits GPS + BeiDou) |
| `spacing_s` | `gnss_rate_s` × `epoch_decimation_n` (0 at the native rate) |
| `on("k")` | key present and not false, 0 or empty |
| `val("k", d)` | the key's value, else `d`, which must be the C++ loader default |

Only these, `v` / `found` (in `armed_when`) and plain operators parse.

**`armed_when`** replaces truthiness where it misreads: a string enum with a truthy default
(`ambiguity_vehicle` `"level"`), a list, a threshold, or a set of keys (the exact update with
`clock_anchor_guard_space` phase and `rank_gate_tol` 1e-11). It sees `v` and `found`.

**`fingerprinted`** marks a key of the warm-resume fingerprint (`networkSnapshotFingerprint`,
`src/core/app/NetworkSnapshotFingerprint.cpp`). CHAIN rule: a resumed leg cannot adopt one; arming it
hard-aborts the leg under `state_snapshot_require` and otherwise silently cold-starts it as a
clean-looking resume (invariant #4), so it is armed in a fresh lineage, and both readouts print a
CHAIN line on a warm config. The posture is fresh convergence only; `ctu_switch_on_resume` (the
switch-on seam, failed card CTU2's G5) stays off.

**The `on()` trap.** `on()` is false for an absent key, so it inverts on a key whose loader
default is true (once every parallel predicate, through `parallel_stations`); such a key takes
`val("k", True)`, and `--verify-loader` fails on `on()` of one.

**Bundles** (`networkPostureBundles_`): keys correct only together; an engaged bundle partially
armed logs a warning. Members need no RECOMMEND_* row.

| bundle | engaged when | members | goldens |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| zero-q phase memory + slip response | `phase_leveling` or `carry_invariant` | `cycle_slip.mw_detect`, `cycle_slip.if_phase_td` | complete |
| armed QC set | `raim` | `raim_attribution`, `raim_within_epoch`, `clock_runaway_check` | complete |
| SH-VTEC / W6 slice | `estimate_iono_sh` | `ionosphere_weighting.enable`, its cutoff ≥ 25 deg, `estimate_sat_code_bias` | not engaged (no SH) |

## Notes

1. Grade datum-invariant quantities on the cold 6 h consumer and the 16-site census against a
   same-exe control ([NETWORK_REFOUNDING](refound/NETWORK_REFOUNDING_2026-09-14.md),
   [MSC](record/refound/MSC_multi_site_consumer_2026-09-21.md)). With `carry_invariant` on, a
   `parallel_carry` result also reproduces only at the same thread count (the fold combines
   per-thread partials in thread-count order). On the float-vehicle goldens the fold holds no
   block (run-start `dd_prior_block`, blocks 0), and on the field golden `parallel_carry` off and
   `parallel_max_threads` 6 to 20 left products and traces byte-identical (card NSP section 7),
   so the thread count is not a grading condition there.
2. The loader overrides config values, so the run manifest and load log record what ran:
   `raim_within_epoch` arms `raim` + `raim_attribution`, `phase_leveling` without a detector arms
   `cycle_slip.mw_detect`, code-bias estimation turns
   `model_corrections.enable_code_phase_bias` off, `service_mode` overrides explicit false values
   (`GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp`).
3. `filter.process_noise.clock_bias_q` is inert on the network producer (the receiver clock is a
   free per-epoch column; measured byte-identical, `GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp`).
4. Post-fit `raim` and the clock monitor run after emission and protect the next epoch;
   `raim_within_epoch` alone adapts an epoch before it is emitted (`ApplicationNetwork.cpp`).
5. Only the health trace is flushed during a run; other traces close at teardown, so a killed run
   loses their tails and `trace_flag_rates` writes nothing (`NetworkRunTraces.cpp`).
6. A producer and its consumers share one wind-up mode (the census gain is the accumulating
   consumers' own), and a sim fixture sits on the producer's side of the wind-up sign fix
   ([WUP](record/refound/WUP_windup_accumulated_promotion_2026-10-01.md),
   [wind-up sign](record/cards/network-vehicle/WINDUP-SIGN-DEFECT.md)).
7. Golden arms are GPS + Galileo since A16: `rapid_arm.py score` pools E5a with band 5,
   `ftc_step_attrib.py` R3 needs `--systems G,E`, and `census6h.py` / `simcensus6h.py` default
   to GPS-only bases where `gcensus.py` does not ([GAD](record/refound/GAD_galileo_adoption_2026-10-13.md)).
8. The sim golden starts inside the fixture's start artifact, so the sim is graded from 00:30:15
   ([EXL](record/refound/EXL_exact_update_lah_promotion_2026-10-01.md) section 4).

| `network.epoch_decimation_n` | 1 | 1 | `True` | Epoch decimation, estimation-relevant and fingerprinted: n=1 for any graded run, n=2 the 60 s dev tier, n=10 emission mechanics only (carry prediction invalid). Goldens 1. | [SPEC_dev_tier_perf_cards](record/SPEC_dev_tier_perf_cards.md) |
| `network.parallel_carry` | false | true | `par` | Threads the dense per-block algebra of the invariant carry (`network.carry_invariant`, off in both goldens): the DD-prior fold, `buildDdPriors` and `installPosterior`. On the float-vehicle goldens the fold holds no block (run-start contributions table: `dd_prior_block`, blocks 0), and turning the key off leaves products, `network_events.csv`, `closed_loop_trace.csv`, `network_epoch_status.csv` and `network_solution.csv` byte-identical (card NSP section 7: 2026-10-28 field golden, DOY 129 and 130, 6 h). History on the invariant carry: 2.13x wall clock at 20 threads, reassociating to ~1e-4 at the solution, so a reference held only at the same thread count. Pinned true in both goldens. |  |
| `network.parallel_max_threads` | 0 | 20 | `par and on("network.parallel_carry")` | Thread cap of the parallel family (0 = all cores). On the float-vehicle goldens it changes no output: caps 6, 8, 10, 12 and 16 against the pinned 20 left products, `network_events.csv`, `closed_loop_trace.csv`, `network_epoch_status.csv` and `network_solution.csv` byte-identical (card NSP section 7: 2026-10-28 field golden, DOY 129 and 130, 6 h). Per-station and per-block work is combined in a fixed order, and the carry fold whose partials combine in thread-count order holds no block there. History on the invariant carry: counters and hashes depended on the count, so a pinned N kept them comparable. Goldens pin 20. |  |
| `network.parallel_min_stations` | 10 | 8 | `pstations and n_sta >= 4` | Strict roster-size gate (n_sta > value) for the whole parallel family, silent when it closes; goldens pin 8, below the 59/60-station rosters. |  |
| `network.product_flush_per_epoch` | false |  | `False` | Per-epoch flush of the product file for a live tail consumer; byte-neutral by construction, armed by service_mode, nothing gained in batch runs. Unset in both goldens. |  |
| `network.trace_dd_fractional` | false | true | `not rt and n_sta <= 100` | Writes network_dd_fractional.csv, the input of every DD/AR grade (grade_ddfrac, the ref-free re-reference tools); costly at scale. Pinned on in both goldens. |  |
| `network.trace_dd_fractional_max_pairs` | 0 | 2000 | `on("network.trace_dd_fractional") and n_sta >= 100` | Seeded per-epoch subsample cap on DD-trace pairs (0 = unlimited); unbounded, the trace cost 20-40 min/epoch at 305 stations. Goldens pin 2000. |  |
| `network.trace_dd_fractional_ref` | lowest |  | `on("network.trace_dd_fractional")` | Reference-satellite rule of the DD trace (lowest \| oldest); oldest is quieter as traced but 2-3x above the ref-free grade. Goldens keep lowest under a documented continuity exception. | [DD-TRACE-REF-SAT](record/cards/sim-bed/DD-TRACE-REF-SAT.md) |

| `network.phase_families` | [] | ["E:L1X>L1C", "E:L5X>L5Q"] | `on("network.ring_fix.enable")` | The roster's phase-family table (card NRF section 112; section 109's points 1 to 3 with the managing session's amendments 1, 3, 4 and 5, section 109.1): entries "<constellation>:<member>><identity>" ("E:L1X>L1C", "E:L5X>L5Q"). At every producer station a member's phase, Doppler and signal-strength codes are read under the identity's names (the parse of the station's file), so the estimator sees one family per band: one satellite phase bias, one chain group, one ring-fix family; the codes keep their names and their own code biases, and the held-out consumer is read as recorded. The slip layer pairs a family phase with the receiver's own member code when the identity's code is absent (L1C with C1X). At emission each member's P record repeats its identity's (value, flags, hold generation; released with it), declared by a legend note; the rows are fingerprinted for warm resume; a run-start line prints the watched pairs at a receiver of each tracking mode; a run-end line counts the member records. Empty is off and byte-identical. Built 2026-11-17 after the relabel test of section 107 and the slip blind spot of section 107.6. Graded 2026-11-18 (sections 112.1 and 112.2; verified on the sim runs f40_h40off, f40_m1ta, f40_s_on and f40_s_rl and the observed runs n41_n146, n41r_n146 and n41_n147 with their users, exe nrf41): the simulator bars pass (off identical, no wrong integer, no link held through an injected slip); on observed data bars 1, 2, 3 and 7 pass, 4, 5 and 6 fail (the two-run audit 2 163 failing Galileo checks in hours 2 to 8, the users at JPLM, MRC1 and SEYG about a thousand near-null pair-epochs a day), not adopted. A member-counter defect found there is fixed (nrf42). To do: trace the early root-dependent steps and the pilot receivers' near-null steps that keeping the code biases apart brings back (section 110.3's run A, codes merged, has neither). 2026-11-18: RECOMMEND_SCOPED, with network.phase_family_codes true, in the integer-fixing chain configuration on branch chain/audit (the author's decision of about 16:45, card NRF section 123; the main reference configurations unchanged). Verified by section 118.1 on DOY 146 and 147 (exe nrf46; annotated by sections 107.12, 118.3 and 118.4): C1 to C3 and the network part of C4 pass, lost satellite-samples 8, 4 and 7 against today's 495, 431 and 415 and the two-run audit with 0 failing Galileo checks; C4's users miss: the paired per-receiver difference above 1 mm in some hours in six of the eight groups (means of the hourly medians +0.09 to +0.65 mm, untraced) and three short runs wrong by the float check, two of them new on GPS (QUIN G10–G02, 14 epochs; ASCG G20–G23, 16 epochs). Section 121's stage A not run, by the author's decision; stage R (section 121.3) read that the pair tree alone removes the lost class. To do: (a) the adopted configuration in full on five days (section 123's read, bars F1 to F6); (b) the users' millimetre cost traced; (c) the two wrong GPS runs traced; (d) the bias between the two signal types modelled (section 107.8's per-make code term), in the native build of the family; (e) the streamed ingest, refused with the table (field-blocking for a real-time network). Inert on the simulator (the fixture holds Galileo C1C/L1C and C5Q/L5Q only). | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part18.md) |

| `network.ring_fix.resume_response` | off | release | `on("network.ring_fix.enable")` | The response of a held link to a tracking break of its own station and satellite (day program of 2026-11-19, package held-resume-release; BRV findings R2 and R3; BRV decision 3). off (default): none, byte-identical. release: a gap resumption of the link's own station and satellite whose level is shifted or not measured on the link's pair (or one of its signals) releases the hold in its epoch, before PASS B, cause resume, carried entries re-opened. revalidate: it suspends the hold (not held, carried entries re-opened, the integers kept in a crossing record) until the link's own float accepts them at the level kRingFixResumeLevel 1e-3 (chi-square, formal covariance times sigma_scale^2) with the odds rule deciding them for kRingFixPersistEpochs decision epochs (held again, no window), rejects them while the odds rule decides others, or the decision window kRingFixWindowS passes; a slip, a chain event, a cut or a new break end it. No fixed size enters the response: the classes are the slip layer's (its empirical per-station gates) and the re-validation is a test in the float's own scale. A clean measured resumption keeps the hold under both values. RINGFIX release (cause resume), resume_suspend and resume_clear events; run-end counters by class with their closure. Read only with network.ring_fix.enable. Built 2026-11-19 on branch chain/line-a-2026-11-19. Armed 2026-11-20 at release in the two primary reference files (golden_field_refound, golden_sim_refound) by the author's arm rule (arm improvements unless measured harmful) and his words of 2026-11-19 22:10 'this is a good time for adoption, merge, and longer runs', announced to him twice by the manager on 2026-11-20 without objection; the frozen rf0hv18 files and the BeiDou file keep it off. Verified by: the six observed days of 2026-11-19 at release against same-engine key-off runs (24 h; every epoch emitted; held share identical by hour, largest difference +0.1 point; the five recorded cases BOGT00COL G09, POTS00DEU G13, KIT300UZB G29 twice and G10 released at their return epochs; no hold folded above 0.03 m at a shifted or unmeasured resumption against 5 with the key off; products identical before each day's first resume event); the simulator break beds (truth-wrong held link-epochs 2 to 0) and the bed of the POTS00DEU G13 shape (8 to 0, and the key-off run's second wrong decision removed); the injected beds where the slip layer measures the break identical at all values; off identity on six controls; unit suite RingFixResume. To check: revalidate on the simulator only; the 6 h simulator beds of NRF section 122 at night; days beyond the six adopted ones. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part19.md) |

| `estimation.cycle_slip.lli_slip_mask` | 7 | 3 | `False` | Which bits of a phase observation's loss-of-lock indicator the Melbourne-Wubbena slip detector reads as a loss of lock (the users' engine, MwSlipDetector::hasAnyPhaseLockLoss and the two LLI band memories): 7, the default, any bit (the detector as built); 3, bit 0 (lock lost) and bit 1 (half-cycle ambiguity) only. RINEX 3 defines bit 2 for Galileo as BOC tracking of an MBOC signal, not a loss of lock; the Leica receivers of the roster (HKSL, REYK, WTZR, WROC) raise it on every Galileo L1C phase, so under 7 every Galileo ambiguity resets at every epoch there and the between-satellite reference changes with it. The network's slip scan reads bit 0 only. Adopted 2026-11-16 for the users of the chain posture with network integer fixing (their own configurations), on the requesting session's decision for the author's review, although three of its bars (2 to 4) failed on DOY 145: the reading with 3 is the RINEX definition, no hold is wrong, and Galileo alone at those receivers goes from 0.1 to 1.0 m to 8 to 34 mm. | `files.use_bias_file` | true | true | Whether PathResolver returns the observation day's bias file (.bia / .bsx) with the other products. True (the default) is the behaviour before the key: the file is globbed and read, and although the reference files apply none of its values (model_corrections.enable_code_phase_bias false), its SATELLITE_CLOCK_REFERENCE_OBSERVABLES lines, its coverage and the seeds still reach the run. False: no .bia and no .bsx is returned in either data mode (internal: not globbed; production: removed from the downloader's list); a bias file named in files.product_list_path with false is a configuration error. Built 2026-11-23 for the author's ruling of that morning to drop the CODE bias file from the reference (card NBF); unit-tested (suite PathResolverBiasFile). Armed 2026-11-23 as false in the reference configurations golden_field_refound and golden_sim_refound only, with model_corrections.bias_coverage_requires_apply true, by the author's ruling of that morning ('Yes, drop the CODE bias file from the reference'); the frozen lineages *_rf0hv18 and the BeiDou lineage keep the default (the file is read). Verified by: card BRP (branch card/brp-broadcast-poster, not merged): the network without the file on day 129 at 6 h holds the same share of integers (within 0.4 point), 0 different of 224,104 compared in the cycle form, 298 marks against 1,053; the simulator and 2026 day 145 at 2 h; 48 receiver-days of 2024 with the user key, median change +0.01 mm, none worse than 9.6 mm on the 6 h median, SCRZ day 130 better by 122.5 mm. Card NBF step 2: at the default the engine is identical to main's on both reference files over 2 h (products, every trace, the run manifest), and false on the unchanged data tree is identical in products and every trace to card BRP's data copy without the file (gates/adopt_2026-11-23/nbf_identity_{f,s}.txt, nbf_equivalence_f.txt) (basic). Card NBF step 4 (6 h on the engine of 89b5148, against the adopted baselines _rap_a22h): every epoch emitted on days 129, 130, 132, the simulator and 2026 day 145; the simulator 406,385 held link-epochs right, 0 wrong; held integers identical in the cycle form on days 129, 132, 145 and the simulator; marks 298 against 1,053 on day 129, equal elsewhere; held share within 0.5 point except day 130, GPS hour 6, -1.17 point; on day 130 two GPS links (KOKV00USA G31, GAMB00PYF G08) are held one narrow-lane cycle from the control, and on both the run without the file fits worse by 2 to 4 mm (each decided early on a marginal float the control refused); built-in users within 2 cm by hour; the 48 receiver replays of days 129, 130 and 132 on the new products: median change +0.7, -0.0 and -1.6 mm over hours 2 to 6, none worse than 10.3 mm, SCRZ day 130 better by 120.8 mm (gates/adopt_2026-11-23/REPORT_nbf_step4.md, REPORT_nbf_day130.md). To do: On day 130 the reference without the bias file holds two wrong network integers, each one narrow-lane cycle: KOKV00USA G31 from 04:02 and GAMB00PYF G08 from 03:03 to the end of the 6 h run (235 and 352 held link-epochs, 587 of the run's 289,065 held GPS and Galileo link-epochs; the cycle form compared 184,716 link-epochs, 1,025 different, all from these two), decided at a fraction of 0.19 and 0.20 cycle with a stated sigma of 0.110 and 0.120 where the adopted reference refused them for 45 to 100 minutes and then held the neighbouring narrow lane; no receiver of the 16 is worse by 1 cm. Decisions at a fraction of 0.19 or more with a stated sigma of 0.10 or more are routine in both runs (14, 11, 12, 3 and 10 on days 129, 130, 132, 2026 day 145 and the simulator, most taken by the adopted reference at the same epoch with the same integers); only these two led to other integers. SCRZ day 130's integers. | [NBF-NO-BIAS-FILE-REFERENCE](cards/network-vehicle/NBF-NO-BIAS-FILE-REFERENCE.md) |

| `estimation.ambiguity_resolution.wl_branch_check` | false | true | User AR: the wide-lane integer of a satellite pair is decided by the code and the ionosphere-free phase together. A pair the code refuses is admitted when the phase of the iono-free row is decisive for W0, or for W1 when the code cannot exclude W1 (\|r\| + 3 sigma_n <= half the branch gap, 0.471 cycle for GPS L1/L2, 0.051 for Galileo E1/E5a, where the phase is never decisive at a 0.04 floor); three integers within reach of the code sigma are refused; a pair the code admits at W0 while the phase is decisive for W1 is a contradiction: neither integer is taken, and once the phase has persisted (wl_branch_persist_s) the satellites at fault are returned and have their receiver-satellite code-bias states reset to the prior when that family is on. A decision by the phase and a contradiction take effect only after persistence. Needs wl_from_float and nl_iono_free_row (inert with a warning otherwise). Default off and byte-identical off. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). Look here: the run-end line 'Wide-lane branch check' (evaluated, decided by the phase, persisted contradictions, waiting, refused for reach, satellites returned) and the WLBRANCH lines; 0 decided by the phase on a run with the key on and float pairs beyond 0.25 cycle means inert wiring. Pinned 2026-11-19 in the reference configurations golden_field_refound and golden_sim_refound only (card IRM, fixing user), by the author's ruling of that evening ('Yes, fixing user'): the held-out user of the reference is the fixing user, configured as the users of the adopted days (runs_nom/_rap_nw_obsadx_146_utqi/arm.json); the frozen lineages *_rf0hv18 and the BeiDou lineage keep the float undifferenced user. Requires network.emit_phase_frame_hold; outside the goldens' regime; arming is the author's decision. | [ICL3](record/refound/ICL3_frame_quorum_2026-11-05.md) |

CHEND; network/state/ChainMemory.hpp): the chain's tree memory (the vertices seen in earlier trees, the last tree roots, the root row's pinned targets, the pinned lives, the anchor ages, the emitter's anchor baseline), the leveling carries' re-level stamps, the satellites' code-reference events, the ring fix's links and maps with its two hand-offs, the emitter's held-value state (step declaration, unsettled steps, continuity guard) and the group-delay latch with its window, for network.resume_chain_restore. | `network.ring_fix.cascade` | [] |  | A third signal's integer from a held pair (card NRF part 8, the requesting session's order B after section 60, the step after the log arm t3l): on every link the pair holds, the third signal's integer is decided against the pair's held integer of its reference (the pair's signal on the third's band, a same-frequency sibling such as GPS L1W from L1C, else the one nearest in frequency, GPS L5 from L2W, Galileo E5b from E5a) from their float difference, then held; released with the pair, at a slip of the third signal, across a cut of its band, by its monitor or after a gap. The third signal is decided by its cascade only. With it the settled flags are kept per family. Entries "<c>:<s1>/<s2>><s3>"; each pair must be one of network.ring_fix.pairs. Empty is off, byte-identical. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part8.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.cascade_log` | false |  | The cascades decide and trace, never hold (card NRF part 9, section 77): a link whose third-signal integer passes the gate for the persistence is traced as would_hold at every such epoch, with truth on the simulator, and never enters the hold table, so the third signal stays float. For the read of the would-be decisions on observed data that comes before any hold there. Needs network.ring_fix.cascade. False is off, byte-identical. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part9.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.cascade_rehost_keep` | false |  | A chain re-host of a cascade's third band onto a link the cascade holds, whose third-signal carry sits on the held integer, takes it at a step of 0 and keeps the moved side's carries, as network.ring_fix.rehost_keep_carry does for the pairs' held links (card NRF part 9, order K2 after section 77.1: with the third band's tree on the pair's forest every third-band re-host was a cut release, and the persistence after those releases was what remained refused). Needs network.ring_fix.cascade. False is off, byte-identical. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part9.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.cascade_tree` | false |  | A cascade's third band grows its tree on its pair's forest (card NRF part 9, section 77, approved after section 76): the chain's walk of the third band takes first the arcs that are tree edges of both bands of the pair, then the links the pair holds (those already in the band's tree first), then the band's own incumbents and new edges, so that its tree paths close over arcs whose slant ionosphere the pair's integers fix. On the simulator every fraction and odds refusal of the cascade sat on a link whose third band's path left the pair's holds (section 76). Needs network.ring_fix.cascade. False is off, byte-identical. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part9.md) |

| `network.ring_fix.continuity_guard_cyc` | 0 |  | The stream's continuity guard (card NRF part 10, sections 89 and 90): per held family and satellite the emitter forms the satellite's ionosphere-free level in narrow-lane cycles from its own records (clock, the two phase biases, the two code biases of the same bands) less the family's running median epoch-to-epoch step; while both signals stay flagged and neither counter steps, a level that moves from its value at the run's start by more than this many cycles lowers both flags for network.ring_fix.continuity_guard_hold emissions and steps both counters (the unsettled steps then step them while lowered and once more when the flags rise), so a user that waits for the flags never fixes on a level the stream calls continuous while it moves. 0 is off and byte-identical; in (0, 1]; needs network.ring_fix.unsettled_steps; read only with network.ring_fix.enable. Built 2026-11-16 after DOY 146's flagged Galileo X/X levels moved 1.4 to 1.6 cycles within runs of constant counters (section 83.2); its offline twin stream_guard.py is graded with the users in section 89. | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part10.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.form_configured_pairs` | false |  | The chord table forms every family of network.ring_fix.pairs on an arc that tracks both its signals, beside the rule that pairs every band-1 signal with the arc's lowest-digit signal of another band (card NRF part 8 section 62, part 7 section 60 item 0: without it a family of a third band, Galileo L1C/L7Q or GPS L1C/L5Q, has no chords where the pair's second band is tracked). The first station parameter added through the network's own parameter set (card NOM5 step N6h); the single-receiver flag estimation.estimate_tropo_gradients sets only the consumer's layout. Built default off and byte-identical off apart from the declared run-start rows of the new kind; fingerprinted (token tgrad:1). Graded by card NOM5 gate G4 (2026-10-30, section 4.4): the station blocks stay well conditioned (no block refused or dropped), but the uncarried columns fit noise at their formal sigma (5 to 7 mm) on the sim bed, which has no gradient, and at HKSL00HKG they took up a station-level step (gradient 390 mm, other arcs' carried levels moved by metres, the attribution refloat not tripped): the columns unbounded are not field-runnable. | `network.rx_gf_datum` | prior_refold |  | How the receiver geometry-free direction is held (one per receiver and constellation: its slant ionospheres, DCB, extra-band IFB and phase biases moving together, invisible to the data). prior_refold (default) folds network.rx_code_bias_datum_sigma_m on every epoch on top of the carried posterior, byte-identical (the reported DCB sigma is then 10/sqrt(n)); birth_prior folds it only where the DCB has no carried row this epoch (a birth, a return after the 900 s gap prune, an erase); hard places a row of weight 1e10 (the chain pins' weight) on the DCB of every (receiver, constellation), roots included, at 0 where uncarried and at the carried value otherwise, with RX_GF_DATUM place, reseed and release events and a run-end line. Read only with network.rx_code_bias_datum. The third-band datum record XBD (day program 2026-11-19, package systems-extra-band-datum) proves by exact rank, on toy networks and the real _rap_k1t_n144 roster, that one DCB row per (receiver, constellation) at every receiver closes this direction as a pure datum on dual and triple frequency (JNM decision 1). Not fingerprinted yet: the snapshot fingerprint file belongs to another line of work (token owed). Built 2026-11-19, default off. | `estimation.ambiguity_resolution.extra_signals` | false |  | User AR: a satellite pair tracked on more than two signals is fixed instead of skipped. The two signals on the constellation's primary bands (primary_bands) go through the wide-lane and N_1 step exactly as a two-signal pair does; every other signal gets an extra-wide lane against the lower primary signal (e = n_lo - n_x, admitted when \|e - round(e)\| + 3 sigma_e <= wl_max_frac, no frequency-difference division so no amplification guard) and, only when the primary pair is fixed, the integer N_x = N_lo - round(e) as one more pseudo-observation row with the same hold variance. With hold_release_cyc the held extra-wide lane is compared with the float and a contradiction releases the satellite through the existing path. Needs wl_from_float (inert with a warning otherwise). Default off and byte-identical off (state, ambiguity, residual and closed-loop traces, both engines). Run once on data as a demonstration, no bar (card ARN section 13: CODE products with one fitted bias per satellite and extra signal, three receivers, one day): the extra-signal integers are fixed (Galileo E5b and E5 AltBOC held on 98.7 to 99.8 % of pair-epochs) and none differs between two start times; the position gains nothing and is 4 to 13 mm worse with GPS L5 pinned (Block IIF L5 phase does not follow one bias per day). The pinned row has no weight for the extra signal's bias uncertainty. Look here: the run-end line 'Extra signals' (evaluated, admitted, fixed, held pairs released by an extra-wide lane), the EWLFLOAT lines and the HOLDREL lines with d_ewl; 0 admitted with float extra-wide lanes within 0.1 cycle of an integer means inert wiring. | [ARN-USER-FIXING-ON-NETWORK-PRODUCTS](record/cards/user-ar/ARN-USER-FIXING-ON-NETWORK-PRODUCTS.md) |
| `estimation.ambiguity_resolution.primary_bands` | {"GPS": "12", "Galileo": "15", "BeiDou": "26"} |  | User AR: per constellation (name as the code spells it), the two RINEX band digits of the primary signals, the bands the phase-bias products give biases for; they carry the wide-lane and N_1 step, every other signal of a satellite pair is an extra signal. A given object replaces the default table; a constellation without an entry keeps the rule that a pair needs exactly two signals. An entry that is not two distinct digits is refused with a warning (the default entry of that constellation is kept, or the entry dropped when it has none). Read only with extra_signals. Run at its default in the demonstration of card ARN section 13. | [ARN-USER-FIXING-ON-NETWORK-PRODUCTS](record/cards/user-ar/ARN-USER-FIXING-ON-NETWORK-PRODUCTS.md) |
| `estimation.ambiguity_resolution.wl_branch_persist_s` | 120 |  | Seconds the ionosphere-free phase must stay decisive for the same wide-lane integer of the same satellite pair (satellite, reference, both signals) before wl_branch_check acts on a decision by the phase or on a contradiction. A one-epoch float transient (a change of reference satellite, a hold on other pairs) with a formal sigma too small by a factor of ten once made the phase decisive for a wrong neighbouring integer on real data; the persistence lets it pass. The run restarts on a change of integer, on an epoch where the phase is decisive for neither integer, and after a gap longer than the larger of this value and 1 s; 0 acts at once. Refused when negative (warning, default kept). Read only with wl_branch_check. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). Look here: the 'waiting' count of the run-end line 'Wide-lane branch check' and the persist field of the WLBRANCH lines. | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `estimation.cycle_slip.graphic_trend_centred` | off |  | User-path GRAPHIC slip detector (estimation.cycle_slip.graphic, off by default) tests the change of dG from one epoch to the next minus its window median instead of \|dG\|. The default \|dG\| holds the range rate, so on a moving satellite it exceeds its threshold at arc epoch 6 and resets the arc over and over; the centred test moves the output of the user-gate arms that set graphic (u_rtk_te_gr, UA8). Graded only on the 600 s gate arm, never against truth or field data; the network does not read it. | [CAF-COMMENT-AUDIT-FINDINGS](record/cards/queues/CAF-COMMENT-AUDIT-FINDINGS.md) |
| `estimation.estimate_rx_sat_code_bias` | false |  | The receiver's deviation from the published satellite code bias as a state of the single-receiver filter, one per (satellite, raw code signal) on a signal whose published bias is applied (.BIA or SSR), +1 on the uncombined raw code row, never pruned, random walk on every predict, kept in the between-satellite difference for every satellite. Uncombined processing only: the loader turns it off with a warning otherwise, and the network producer and the baseline layout never carry it. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). Look here: the run-end line 'receiver-satellite code bias' (born, learned, reset, written counters); 0 born on a run with the key on means inert wiring. | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `estimation.rx_sat_code_bias_store` |  |  | Path of the JSON store that carries the learned receiver-satellite code biases from one post-processing run to the next: read at the start when the file exists and its receiver type, receiver version and bias-source series equal the run's (otherwise ignored, logged and counted), written at the end (learned states only, zero mean per constellation and signal; entries the run never saw are carried over). The real-time engine neither reads nor writes it. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `estimation.rx_sat_code_bias_systems` | ["GPS"] |  | Constellations that get receiver-satellite code-bias states, named as the code spells them (GPS, Galileo, GLONASS, BeiDou, QZSS, SBAS); an unknown name is dropped with a warning. GPS only by default: the float wide lane shows a satellite constant at the Trimble and the Javad and none worth a state for Galileo. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `filter.initial_std.rx_sat_code_bias` | 0.15 |  | Prior standard deviation (m) of a receiver-satellite code-bias state: the measured per-satellite spread of the float wide lane (0.095 cycle at the Javad, 0.138 at the Trimble) over 0.773 cycle per metre. Also the scale of the learned test (a state is learned at or below half this sigma). Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `filter.process_noise.rx_sat_code_bias_q` | 1e-10 |  | Random-walk spectral density (m2/s) of a receiver-satellite code-bias state, applied on every predict to every state of the family whether or not its satellite is in view: 3 mm in a day against a measured day-to-day difference of 0.010 to 0.015 cycle on a pair. Also the growth of a stored entry's variance over its age. Built, unit-tested, never run on data (no graded run; the card's bars are proposed, not frozen). | [RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS](cards/user-ar/RSCB-USER-RECEIVER-SATELLITE-CODE-BIAS.md) |
| `realtime.state_snapshot_latch_capture` | false |  | Writes the initializer's four seed latches (I rows) into every consumer snapshot the engine writes, for state_snapshot_latch_restore; the rows change no estimate; armed by the event harness on its seeds (card CRS2). | [CRS2](record/refound/CRS2_resume_hold_and_latches_2026-11-06.md) |
| `realtime.state_snapshot_latch_restore` | false |  | A restored consumer keeps the seed latches its snapshot holds (the code-bias latch under the full scope only), so it does not re-seed the restored inter-system bias and receiver code biases from one epoch of code (14 m on the GPS + Galileo golden, card CRS); armed by the event harness on its legs, graded there (card CRS2). | [CRS2](record/refound/CRS2_resume_hold_and_latches_2026-11-06.md) |
| `realtime.state_snapshot_path` |  |  | RT / consumer filter-state snapshot for a warm start; landed as the event-harness consumer default (card CC), but no benefit graded on a continuous product stream; seams are a scope-cut add-back. | [CC-declared-step-carry](record/cards/consumer/CC-declared-step-carry.md) |
| `realtime.tcp_silent_timeout_s` | 0 |  | Redials the primary raw-TCP source after this many seconds without a byte; a peer that hangs with its socket open used to keep the stream silent for good. Default 0 is off. Unit-tested against a loopback server, never run on a live receiver. | [CAF-COMMENT-AUDIT-FINDINGS](record/cards/queues/CAF-COMMENT-AUDIT-FINDINGS.md) |

| `network.apriori_clock_track_phase_clock_hold` | false |  | Card BRD2: with network.apriori_clock_track under sp3_brdc_clock, admits the phase-clock hold (network.emit_pb_hold_phase_clock) in place of the smoothed hold; within a generation the delivered phase-visible clock is then the tracked a priori brdc(iode) + O + U, the sum of the per-epoch IF-TD columns, whose error accumulates. | `network.ring_fix.nn_guard` | false |  | The near-null guard (card NRF section 108): each held Galileo E1/E5a chord keeps a moving average of the geometry-free and ionosphere-free combinations of its two post-fit code residuals; after each solve, centred on its satellite's family and then its receiver, a deviation within nn_guard_tol_gf_m of k near-null steps (k (4 lambda2 - 3 lambda1), about 0.448 m, the code signature of a pair held k (3, 4) off) with the ionosphere-free deviation within nn_guard_max_if_m moves the chord's pair by -k (3, 4) and re-opens its carried entries so the hold rows re-level it; a second flag during the same hold releases it; tree edges are not moved. RINGFIX nn_move and nn_release events; run-end counters. False is off and byte-identical; read only with network.ring_fix.enable. Built 2026-11-17 after the code signature traced the users' near-null wide lanes to steps the network holds on its own links (sections 107.2, 107.3, 107.7); pre-registered in section 108 with bars on the simulator beds and DOY 146 and 147. KEEP_OFF since 2026-11-17: the simulator bar S3 failed (section 108.1; verified on the sim runs m1_nn, d1_nn, d31_nn, h1_nn and h40_nn against m1_off37, d1_off, d31_off, h1_off and h40_off, exe nrf38, whose guard-off run h40_offn reproduces h40_off of exe nrf37): 12 of its 13 moves took a chord the truth held right to a pair one near-null step off, and the wrong held link-epochs rose 51 % on d1 and 27 % on d31; a constant code offset with a small ionosphere-free part and a one-step error are the same signature in a link's code residuals. No observed run. To do, the author's decision: the code signature as a monitor only (an event and a counter, no move), or a release on the first flag in place of the move, with its own bars; a phase-side test through the slant ionosphere is the alternative to the code. Stays off in the reference configurations of 2026-11-19 (card IRM): S3 fails on the simulator (card NRF section 108.1). | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part14.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.nn_guard_release` | false |  | The release form of the near-null guard (the managing session's queue item N4; card NRF section 115): a chord the guard flags is released at its first flag (its hold dropped, its integer never moved), in place of the move by -k (3, 4) that failed the simulator bar S3 (section 108.1). Built 2026-11-18, default off and byte-identical; read only with network.ring_fix.nn_guard. Pre-registered on the guard's simulator beds with the same truth bars; observed arms only if every simulator bar passes. 2026-11-18, verified by the truth audit on five simulator beds (card NRF section 115.1): S1, S2 and S4 pass, S3 fails on d31 (3 714 against 3 571 wrong held link-epochs); the 11 releases took 10 right chords and 1 wrong, and every chord held again came back on the same pair. To do: none for this form; the monitor and the third frequency remain. Stays off in the reference configurations of 2026-11-19 (card IRM): S3 fails on d31, 143 more wrong held link-epochs (card NRF section 115.1). | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part17.md) |
| `network.ring_fix.reattach` | false |  | The re-attachment of untied satellites (card NRF part 12, sections 102 and 102.2): after each epoch's settled flags, a satellite of a held family's tree that is not settled and holds no tie (no held chord, no certified tree edge) for 1 800 s, with at least 3 candidate links whose receivers are in the flagged piece and not below the satellite in its tree, whose floats passed the decision's window with an ionosphere-free sigma of at most 0.10 cycle, and whose fractions agree (circular standard deviations at most 0.08 cycle ionosphere-free and 0.10 cycle wide lane) on a common offset of at least 0.10 cycle ionosphere-free or 0.15 cycle wide lane, has its tree edge moved at the next election onto the candidate nearest their medians, in every band of its family: the incumbent edge is not seeded, the chosen arc is a lead arc of rank 1, and the pin is an ordinary re-host (the new host link's rounded float, an unknown step); no integer is decided, and the satellite is not moved again for 3 600 s. RINGFIX untied_move, untied_applied and untied_missed events; run-end counters. False is off and byte-identical; needs network.ring_fix.settled_flags; read only with network.ring_fix.enable. Built 2026-11-17 after a lost satellite's level was traced to the one link of its last re-host (sections 96.1 and 100.1); its first version (600 s, no hold-off, no offset minimum) moved converging satellites again every 11 min on the sim (102.1). KEEP_OFF since 2026-11-17: the amended rule failed its simulator bars S2 and S4 (section 102.3; verified on the sim runs m1_on37 and d1_on against m1_off37 and d1_off, exe nrf37): it moved almost only satellites already settling, each move delaying its satellite 10 to 15 min, and on the per-link code-error bed it moved one satellite in 6 h. Parked by the author on 2026-11-17: no further runs or tuning, on the sim or on observed data. To do, once the wrong X/X holds of section 102.3 are understood (part 13): a bed that produces the defect (a lone host handing a satellite to young links), and the pin-at-re-host form (the median of the satellite's converged candidates in place of one new host link's rounded float when three agree, adding no move and no window restart). | [NRF](refound/NRF_network_ring_fixing_2026-11-14_part12.md) |

| `estimation.ambiguity_resolution.search_unheld_only` | false |  | User AR: the WL-constrained search covers only the candidates that are not yet held, conditioned on the held ones. A candidate is held when both primary signals' DD variances are at most ten times hold_variance (a fix's pseudo-observation leaves the DD at or below the hold variance; floats sit three decades above it). Every candidate held: no search (ending all_held), nothing written. Otherwise the free floats and covariance are conditioned on the held candidates' nearest integers (y_F\|H = y_F - Q_FH Q_HH^-1 (y_H - z_H), Q_F\|H = Q_FF - Q_FH Q_HH^-1 Q_HF, floored covariance) and searched with the ratio threshold, the success rate and the per-pair cap; the ratio upper bound applies only when the conditioned covariance is degenerate (smallest eigenvalue at hold scale carried into the search's cycles), ending degenerate. An accepted search writes rows for the free pairs only: a held pair is never written again and never re-searched, so no hold is re-applied (the release of a wrong pair is unchanged: arc refloat, partner refloat, setting). Default off and byte-identical off. Look here: the run-end line 'Unheld search' (searches, with every candidate held, of the free set, fixed, pairs installed, held candidates conditioning them, would-have-fired count of the bound, degenerate refusals), the UNHELD debug lines and the held census columns of closed_loop_trace.csv; 0 searches of the free set on a run with the key on and satellites rising means inert wiring. Run once on four 24 h replays (card UFQ section 10, simulator, WTZZ day 129, UTQI day 146, CHTI day 143): rise to join 0.3 to 0.5 min sooner, error by hour and calibration unchanged, 1,750 to 2,620 re-confirmation searches a day not run, the day's one wrong hold at UTQI not installed (one marginal event); not to be armed alone. On the poster's 85 warm starts (card UFQ section 12, 2026-11-21; arm1120's networks replayed): times to 10 and 5 cm unchanged, error after the first hour better in 12 starts, worse in 22, equal in 51, no wrong hold; fails the managing session's rule for the poster's users. Kept off for that measured cost. | [UFQ-USER-FIX-QUALITY](record/cards/user-ar/UFQ-USER-FIX-QUALITY.md) |

| `estimation.phase_windup_gap_bridge` | false |  | Carries the accumulated wind-up angle across a data gap of 300 s to under 900 s by walking it through virtual epochs from the orbit products instead of restarting the accumulator and refloating the stepped arcs; refused (restart as before) in the Earth's umbra, in a GPS noon-turn window and on a quarter-cycle step or closure error. Needs estimation.accumulate_phase_windup; default off and byte-identical off. Field golden against same-exe controls, 6 h and a day: no regression (held-out consumer within 0.15 mm in every hour, census median -0.01 mm; day: 267 of 280 gaps bridged, arcs refloated 547 to 23) and no benefit (1 mm needed). An independent recount of the whole-cycle counts equals the bridge's for 91 of 91 listed gaps and 45 of 45 nonzero counts of the day, with the accumulator origin taken from the first carried ambiguity (the receiver-data origin misses one attempt through a fault of the check). Correct and inert on the float vehicle, so KEEP_OFF at the author's decision of 2026-10-31; the simulator cannot grade it, because it injects wind-up through the same function. | [WGB](record/refound/WGB_windup_gap_bridge_2026-10-17.md) |
| `filter.measurement_noise.float_phase_scale` | false |  | User noise: the receiver's own phase factor (phase_scale_measure) multiplies the variance of the phase rows of every satellite whose ambiguities are float (not held at the last integer search, census of the held set) and not in the estimator's tail, once the factor of its constellation is formed; held satellites keep the configured weight. Rationale: a float ambiguity absorbs the slow part of its own satellite's phase error, so the white part is the weight that fits its rows; a held one cannot, and the configured weight is the one that keeps the covariance calibrated (card UFQ section 11: per-receiver factors 0.41 to 1.05, lag-one correlation 0.62 to 0.82; the static per-receiver factor on all rows gave the first-fix and second-hour gains of the borrowed 0.5 with an overconfident covariance). Applied to the undifferenced rows before the between-satellite transform. Each switch of a satellite is an event at both edges (debug lines PHSCALE, counters). Default off and byte-identical off. Look here: the run-end line 'Float phase scale' (switches to the receiver's factor and back, scaled satellite-epochs); 0 switches on a run with the key on means inert wiring. Measured harm 2026-11-21 (card UFQ section 13): on the poster's 85 warm starts (arm1120's networks) time to 10 cm 19.75 to 21.0 min, error after the first hour better in 40 starts and worse in 44, wrong holds at three starts (59 epochs on G L1C/L2W and E L1X/L5X, 34 on E L1C/L5Q; arm1120 none), calibration 1.23 to 1.74; on four 24 h users the first fix at WTZZ00DEU 100.5 to 154 min and its calibration 0.74 to 1.89, the simulator's first fix 139 to 71 min with no wrong fix. The factor forms late and, read from the rows it tightens, runs low (a feedback toward overconfidence). Kept off. | [UFQ-USER-FIX-QUALITY](record/cards/user-ar/UFQ-USER-FIX-QUALITY.md) |
| `model_corrections.apply_phase_shifts` | false |  | Subtracts each file's recorded SYS / PHASE SHIFT value from its phase observations at parse time, to recover a receiver-native convention. Card L2Q (2026-11-06) read the L2C minus L2W fraction from the raw RINEX of 294 stations over DOY 128 to 132: every file is aligned as recorded (579 station-days of 21 receiver types, station offsets at most 0.018 cycle, satellite part at most 0.004), while the records carry 0.00, -0.25 and +0.25 cycle for the same relation (Septentrio; Trimble, Leica and Stonex; JAVAD and Topcon), so ON would un-align the L2C phase at 162 of 294 stations in two opposite directions. Unset in every golden; set false in the two AR sample configs. KEEP_OFF at the author's decision, 2026-11-06. | [L2Q](record/refound/L2Q_l2c_l2w_quarter_cycle_2026-11-06.md) |
| `network.ambiguity_resolution.fixed_cycles.fold` | off |  | Detached hold of the search's fixed cycles (off, observe, emit). D1 and D2 FAILED; the hold neither reaches nor helps the consumer on this posture; kept default off as a measure-only instrument. | [FCFC](record/refound/FCFC_fixed_cycle_ledger_2026-10-11.md) |
